The toner smell is constant. \n\nAnd everyone just stops there to wait for their pitch books, and they just stand around talking about their weekends while I'm trying to work. Dave from compliance spent twenty minutes yesterday going on and on about his daughter's lacrosse tournament. I don't even know the rules of lacrosse. It involves a net on a stick, right? It just seems like a lot of pointless running back and forth in the mud. \n\nBut um... what were you asking? Oh, right, being overwhelmed. No, I wouldn't say I feel that way during the day. I just sort of exist through it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations at work?\n\nPatient: Avoiding things? No, not really. I mean, I do all the modeling and the reports I'm supposed to do. I just... I do it all. Actually, I had to redo a whole set of projections yesterday because the data feed from our vendor was completely corrupted. We use this system, I won't name it, but it costs the firm a fortune and half the time the API just hangs. I was on the phone with their tech support for an hour, and the guy—I think his name was Kevin—sounded like he was eating a sandwich the entire time. I could literally hear him chewing. It was turkey, I'm almost sure of it, you know that specific squishy sound? And he kept asking me to restart my machine, which takes ten minutes because of all our security protocols. They put this new encryption software on our laptops last quarter, and it just drags everything down to a crawl. You try to open Excel and it just sits there spinning... \n\nBut, uh, no, I don't avoid my work. I just do it. It's just... there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding social interactions with your coworkers outside of work?\n\nPatient: Social interactions? Outside of work? I mean... I don't really get invited to much anymore, to be honest. Or if I do, I just say no. It's just... I don't have the energy. \n\nActually, the last time I went to a firm thing, it was a holiday party at this steakhouse downtown. The lighting was so dim you could barely read the menu, and they had these jazz musicians playing right next to our table. I don't know who thought a saxophone during dinner was a good idea, but it was just so loud. And the steak was completely overcooked. I asked for medium rare and it came out gray. Gray meat. And it cost the firm probably eighty dollars a plate. I was sitting next to Sarah from HR, and she spent the entire dinner showing me pictures of her kitchen remodel. She put in this backsplash that was just... these tiny, busy little glass tiles that made my eyes hurt just looking at them on her phone. She said it took the contractor three weeks just to do the grout. Three weeks! Imagine having someone in your house for three weeks just spreading paste on a wall. \n\nBut, um... yeah, I don't really hang out with anyone after hours. I just go home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or interest in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Or, wait, interest in things? I don't know, I guess I haven't really noticed a change. I just eat what I usually eat. Though, actually, the cafeteria downstairs changed their vendor last month. They used to have this decent salad bar, but now it's all these pre-packaged wrap things that are mostly just tortilla and wilted lettuce. I bought a chicken Caesar one on Tuesday, and the dressing was in this tiny plastic pouch that was impossible to tear open. I ended up using my scissors at my desk, and of course, it splattered on my keyboard. Have you ever tried to clean mayonnaise out from between the keys of a laptop? It's practically impossible. You have to use a paperclip and a tissue, and even then, there's a greasy film left behind. I spent half my lunch break just trying to get the 'G' and 'H' keys to stop sticking. And the wrap wasn't even good. The chicken was rubbery. \n\nBut, um... activities I enjoy? I don't really know what I enjoy anymore. I just sort of sit on the couch when I get home.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired during the day?\n\nPatient: Tired? Oh, all the time. I mean, I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I just lie there in the dark, replaying all these client models in my head. Like yesterday, I was trying to figure out this asset allocation for the Miller account, and they have this massive trust structure that is just a nightmare to untangle. Their previous advisor had them in these obscure mutual funds with exorbitant expense ratios. I mean, who puts a conservative growth client in a fund with a 1.5% fee? It's basically robbery. I spent three hours just reading the prospectus for one of them, and the fine print on the redemption fees was buried on page forty-two. Page forty-two! And the font was so small I actually had to go find my reading glasses, which I never usually wear. I bought them at a pharmacy in the airport in Atlanta two years ago when my flight got delayed because of a thunderstorm. The lightning was incredible, though, just lighting up the whole tarmac... \n\nBut anyway, I'll finally drift off and then I'm wide awake again at 3 or 4 in the morning. My stomach gets all tight, like a knot, and I just lie there until my alarm goes off. So by the time I actually go to work, I feel like I never actually rested.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in the past that might be contributing to your current state?\n\nPatient: Traumatic events? Um, no, not really. I mean, nothing like a car crash or a war or anything like that. Just the usual stress, I guess. Though, speaking of stress, the building management decided to test the fire alarms yesterday right in the middle of the afternoon. No warning, just this piercing screech that makes your teeth vibrate. It went on for twenty minutes. And the strobe lights! They installed these new LED strobe lights in the hallways last year, and they are blinding. I was trying to read a quarterly report for the Henderson portfolio, and every three seconds, the whole office would flash white. I had to close my blinds, but the blinds in my office are those cheap plastic vertical ones. Half the slats are missing the little clips at the top, so they just hang there crooked, and they don't even block the light properly. I put in a maintenance request for them six months ago. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's nice enough, but he always smells strongly of peppermint. Like, aggressively peppermint. He told me once he uses Dr. Bronner's soap for everything—washing his hair, doing his laundry, cleaning his floors. He said you have to dilute it, though, or it burns. I don't know why anyone would want to use soap that burns... \n\nBut, um, no, no trauma. Just... just life being exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think is causing you to lie awake at night replaying work scenarios?\n\nPatient: Specific? I don't... I don't think so. There's no one big thing. It's just the accounts, the numbers, the emails. They just loop in my head. Like, I'll be lying there, and suddenly I'm thinking about the font on the quarterly statements. We switched to this new reporting software in January, and the default font is Calibri. I hate Calibri. It just looks so... unfinished. I tried to get the IT department to change the global default to Garamond, because Garamond looks like real money, you know? It has gravity. But the IT guy, Kevin, he told me it would mess up the pagination on the PDF exports. Kevin is always talking about pagination. He wears these novelty ties every Friday. Last week it was a tie with little rubber ducks all over it. And he pairs them with these short-sleeved button-down shirts that are always just a little too tight across the shoulders. I don't understand how someone can work in a corporate environment and think a rubber duck tie is appropriate. It just undermines the whole operation. If a client came in and saw that, they'd probably take their portfolio straight to Morgan Stanley. I mean, we handle millions of dollars, and he's walking around looking like a children's television host...\n\nBut, no, there's no specific reason I'm awake. My brain just won't shut off.\n\nDoctor: Is there something at work that you think is causing you the most stress?\n\nPatient: The most stress? I don't... I mean, I guess it's all just kind of a blur of stress at this point. There's not really one single thing I can point to and say, \"That's it.\" It's just the sheer volume of it all, you know? The endless emails, the market fluctuations, the compliance updates... oh, the compliance updates. They send out these memos every Tuesday morning, and they always use this bright red font for the headers. It's so aggressive. I don't know why they can't just use a nice, calm navy blue. Navy blue is authoritative but not screaming at you. I painted my dining room navy blue last spring. Well, technically it was called \"Midnight Sapphire,\" but it's navy. I bought the paint at this little independent hardware store near my house because I try to support local businesses, but the guy who mixed it didn't seal the lid properly. When I put it in the trunk of my car, it tipped over, and let me tell you, getting Midnight Sapphire out of beige carpet is impossible. I tried everything—club soda, vinegar, this industrial cleaner I ordered online that smelled like pine needles and ammonia. The smell was so strong I had to drive with the windows down for a week, and it was still April, so it was freezing. I ended up just buying a rubber cargo mat to cover the stain. It's black, which clashes with the beige, but what can you do? \n\nBut yeah, work is just... it's just heavy. All of it. I don't think one thing is worse than the rest.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about your job that you think is keeping you awake at night?\n\nPatient: Specific about the job? I... no, I don't think it's any one specific thing. It's just the whole environment, you know? It's like this low-level hum of anxiety that never stops. Actually, speaking of humming, the fluorescent light above my cubicle has been making this awful buzzing sound for three weeks. It's not loud, but it's just this constant *bzzzz* that burrows into your brain. I asked facilities to change the bulb, and they sent up this new kid. I think his name is Tyler. He couldn't have been older than twenty, and he was wearing these enormous headphones around his neck the whole time. Not even listening to them, just wearing them like a necklace. Who does that? And they were those bright green ones, like neon green. I don't understand this obsession with neon colors lately. I was at the grocery store yesterday—the one on 4th Street, not the big one out by the highway—and they had this entire display of neon-colored sports drinks. Blue raspberry, electric lemon... who wants to drink something that looks like antifreeze? I ended up just buying my usual sparkling water, but even that was a hassle because they rearranged the aisles. Why do they always rearrange the aisles? You finally learn where the oatmeal is, and suddenly it's in aisle seven next to the dog food. It makes no sense. I don't even have a dog. My sister has a dog, a golden retriever named Buster, and he sheds everywhere. She brought him over for Thanksgiving last year, and I was finding blonde hairs woven into my area rug until Easter... \n\nBut anyway, no. Nothing specific about the job. Just... everything. It just all stays with me when I close my eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself avoiding going into the office or certain work-related tasks because of feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Avoiding tasks? I... well, things are definitely slipping. My financial models, the slide decks for the presentations... I just can't seem to get them together anymore. I actually got a formal performance warning about it last week. HR handed it to me in this terrible, flimsy manila folder. I really don't understand why corporate offices still use manila folders. The color is just so depressing. It reminds me of the waiting room at my old dentist's office, Dr. Aris. He always had this faint smell of peppermint and latex, and he played smooth jazz in the background. Kenny G, mostly. I absolutely cannot stand the saxophone. It's just so... whiny. My nephew wanted to play the saxophone in middle school, but my sister made him take up the clarinet instead because it was cheaper to rent from the music shop. He quit after three weeks anyway. Kids just don't have any follow-through these days. He spends all his time on his phone now, watching other people play video games on the internet. I don't get that at all. Why watch someone else play a game when you could just play it yourself? \n\nBut... where was I? Right, the warning. Yeah, I'm just not keeping up. And it's not just the office. I used to have things that kept my life outside of work intact, you know? But I've completely stopped showing up for my volunteer teaching, and my Tuesday yoga class, and even my book club. I just don't go anymore. The book club host, Susan, always serves these terribly dry little cucumber sandwiches anyway, and she cuts the crusts off, which just seems incredibly wasteful... but yeah. I guess I am avoiding things. Everything, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep issues and stress at work are affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: My relationships? I... I don't really know. I guess I haven't seen anyone enough lately to even tell. I mean, my sister called me on Sunday, but I just let it go to voicemail. Her voicemails are always so long, anyway. She rambles on about her neighbor's landscaping or whatever new diet she's trying. The last one was the keto diet, I think? She was eating so much cheese, just endless blocks of cheddar. I don't know how anyone's digestion can handle that. I tried to make a cheese fondue once for a New Year's Eve party back in, oh, maybe 2015? I bought this beautiful cast-iron fondue pot, cherry red, very heavy. But I didn't get the heat right, and the cheese just separated into this oily, stringy mess. It was so embarrassing. Everyone pretended it was fine and just dipped their bread in the oil, but I knew. I ended up throwing the whole pot away because I couldn't get the burnt cheese off the bottom. I probably should have soaked it in baking soda, but I was just so frustrated. Baking soda is amazing, really. My grandmother used to keep an open box in the fridge to absorb odors, and I still do that. The yellow box, you know the one. \n\nBut... no, I haven't really noticed anything specific with my family or friends. I just don't have the energy to see them, so there's not much to affect, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning that you think might be causing you to wake up?\n\nPatient: Something specific before I wake up? I... no, not really. I mean, the reason I finally came in to see you is because I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I just lie awake for hours, replaying all my client models in my head. And then, without fail, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning, and my stomach is just tight, like a knot. It feels awful. It actually feels a lot like when I had food poisoning back in 2018. I had gone to this seafood place down by the harbor—the one with the blue awnings, not the red ones. I ordered the scallops, and I knew right away something was off because they smelled a little bit like ammonia, but I didn't want to make a fuss, so I ate them anyway. Big mistake. I spent the next two days curled up on my bathroom floor. The tile was so cold, but it actually felt kind of nice against my cheek. I have this really pretty seafoam green tile in my bathroom. I picked it out myself when I remodeled a few years ago. The contractor tried to talk me into white subway tile because it's \"classic,\" but I think white subway tile is just so boring. It looks like a subway station, obviously. Why would I want my bathroom to look like a public transit stop? The subway is always so dirty anyway, and it smells like ozone and old pretzels. \n\nBut... yeah, I just wake up with that tight stomach, and I can't go back to sleep. And then I have to drag myself to work feeling like I never actually rested at all. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think about right before you wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning?\n\nPatient: Something in particular I think about? I... I don't think so. I'm usually just asleep, and then I'm awake, and my stomach is doing that awful knot thing. It's not like I'm having a specific dream or anything. I don't really dream much anyway. Or maybe I just don't remember them. My friend Sarah used to keep a dream journal. She swore by it. She had this beautiful leather-bound notebook with handmade paper. You know the kind, with the little flower petals actually pressed into the pages? It looked so nice, but it was impossible to write on with a regular pen because the ink would bleed everywhere. She insisted on using a fountain pen, which just seemed incredibly messy to me. Fountain pens always leak in your bag. I ruined a perfectly good silk lining in a purse once because of a leaky pen. It was this gorgeous navy blue tote I got on sale at Nordstrom. I tried everything to get the stain out—hairspray, rubbing alcohol, even that expensive dry-cleaning fluid you can buy in the little bottles—but nothing worked. It just left this huge, dark blue smudge right at the bottom. I ended up having to throw the whole bag away, which was a shame because it had the perfect number of pockets. It's so hard to find a bag with good pockets these days. They either have none at all, and everything just falls into a black hole, or they have too many, and you can never remember where you put your keys. \n\nBut... no, I don't think there's any specific thought that wakes me up. It's just the waking up part that happens.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think is causing you the most anxiety or stress right before you go to bed?\n\nPatient: Right before bed? I... I'm not really sure. I don't think there's one specific thing. I usually just try to read a little bit to get my mind to quiet down, so I'm not really focusing on work or anything. I have this brass reading lamp clamped to my headboard. It's supposed to be one of those touch-activated ones, where you just tap the base and it changes brightness? But it's so finicky. Half the time I tap it and nothing happens, or I barely brush against it with my pillow and it turns up to the absolute brightest setting. It takes those weird little halogen bulbs, too, the ones you aren't supposed to touch with your bare hands because the oils from your skin make the glass shatter or burn out faster. I always have to use a tissue when I change them. I had to go to three different hardware stores just to find the right wattage last month. The one I finally found them at was this tiny mom-and-pop place downtown. It smelled exactly like my grandfather's garage—that very specific mix of sawdust, motor oil, and potting soil. He used to spend hours out there fixing up old radios. He had this massive wooden Zenith radio from the 1930s that he restored completely by hand. The dial lit up with this warm orange glow, and it took a full minute to warm up the tubes before you could hear anything but static. It was beautiful, really. I wonder whatever happened to that radio after he passed. My aunt probably sold it at an estate sale for practically nothing. She was always doing things like that, just getting rid of family history without asking anyone else if they wanted it. \n\nBut... anyway. No, I don't think there's a particular thought that stresses me out right before I go to sleep. I'm just so heavy and tired by that point, I just want the day to be over.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that happens during the day that you think might be contributing to your waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning?\n\nPatient: During the day? I... no, not that I've noticed. I mean, my days are all pretty much the exact same. I just sit at my desk looking at financial models and spreadsheets for eight or nine hours. It's just a blur of numbers, really. The only thing that really stands out is how freezing my office is. Building management refuses to adjust the thermostat on our floor, so I have to keep this huge, chunky wool cardigan draped over my chair. It's this really hideous mustard yellow color. I bought it at a thrift store up in Vermont when I went on a trip with my ex-husband, gosh, maybe ten years ago now. We went up there to look at the fall foliage, which was supposed to be relaxing, but we spent the entire weekend arguing because he absolutely refused to use the GPS on his phone. He bought this massive, fold-out paper map at a gas station and insisted on navigating with that because he said it was more authentic. We ended up getting completely lost on this awful dirt logging road for three hours. The rental car got completely coated in mud. It was a silver sedan, and by the time we got back to the highway, it was entirely brown. I remember the guy at the rental return desk gave us the dirtiest look. I always try to return rental cars clean, you know? It just seems polite. I usually even take out the trash from the little side pockets in the doors. People always leave gum wrappers and old receipts in there. \n\nBut... yeah, sorry. No, there's nothing specific during the day that I think causes the waking up. It's just the same dull routine, and then I still end up awake in the middle of the night regardless.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think about right before you fall asleep that might be related to your waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning?\n\nPatient: Right before I fall asleep? Um, no. I don't really think about much of anything. Like I said, I'm usually just reading until my eyes get too heavy to keep open. I've been trying to get through this biography of Marie Antoinette for, honestly, probably six months now. It's so dense. The author spends like three whole chapters just describing the architecture of Versailles and the specific types of silk they imported for the curtains. Which is interesting, I guess, but it makes me so sleepy. I actually went to Versailles once, right after college. I went backpacking through Europe with my roommate, Claire. We took the train out from Paris, and it was pouring rain the entire day. We didn't even get to see the gardens properly because the mud was so thick it was ruining our shoes. I was wearing these cheap canvas sneakers I bought at a market in London, and the water just soaked right through. My feet were freezing. We ended up spending way too much money on hot chocolate in the cafe just to warm up. They served it in these tiny, delicate porcelain cups with little gold rims. I was terrified I was going to drop it and break it, because my hands were shaking so much from the cold. The hot chocolate was so rich, though, almost like drinking melted candy bars. I haven't had hot chocolate like that since. The powdered stuff you buy at the grocery store here just tastes like dusty sugar water in comparison. You have to use actual milk, not just hot water, but even then, it's never the same. \n\nBut, um... no. To answer your question, there's no specific thought right before I fall asleep. I just kind of drift off while I'm reading, and then a few hours later, my eyes are just wide open again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Right before I fall asleep? Well, I guess... yeah, actually. I came in because I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I'll lie there, and instead of reading, I just start replaying all the client models from the day in my head. Just staring at the ceiling, thinking about the spreadsheets. And then I finally drift off, but I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach completely tight. It's awful. I end up going to work feeling like I never actually rested at all. I have to take the subway to work, and it's always so crowded in the mornings. You're just packed in there like sardines. The other day, this man next to me was holding a cup of coffee and the train jerked, and he spilled it all over the floor. It was one of those iced coffees with the plastic cups, and the lid just popped right off. The ice cubes were sliding all over the floor every time the train stopped or started. Nobody said anything, we all just kind of stared at the ice cubes sliding back and forth. It reminded me of playing air hockey when I was a kid. My brother and I used to go to this arcade down the street from our house on Saturday afternoons. He was always so much better at it than me, he'd slam the puck so hard it would fly off the table and hit someone. He actually chipped a tooth once because he leaned too far over the table and the puck bounced up and hit him right in the mouth. My mother was furious. She made him pay for the dental bill out of his allowance, which took him almost a year to pay off. He was so mad about it, he refused to speak to her for a week. 

But, um, yes. I do think about work right before I fall asleep. I just replay those client models over and over, and then I'm up at 3 AM with my stomach in knots.

Well, it's... it's just that I can't seem to hold onto my thoughts anymore. Like, spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me now take me hours. I just completely lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll sit there and reread the same financial assumptions over and over again, and I still worry I've missed something obvious before I have to do a presentation. It's exhausting. I used to be so good at presentations. I remember this one time, maybe five years ago, I had to present to the board of this huge tech startup. We were in this massive glass conference room on the 40th floor, looking out over the whole city. They had catered lunch, and it wasn't just regular sandwiches, it was this incredible sushi spread. Like, really high-end stuff. I ate so much spicy tuna I thought I was going to be sick before I even stood up to speak. I actually dropped a piece of ginger on my blouse right before we started. It was a white silk blouse, too, the kind you have to dry clean. I tried to dab it with a napkin, but it just left this faint pink smudge right near the collar. I spent the whole presentation trying to angle my body so nobody would see it. The CEO was this guy who wore those expensive rimless glasses, and he kept squinting at me. I thought he was looking at the stain, but it turned out he just needed a new prescription. He told me afterward he was getting laser eye surgery the next week. I've always been terrified of the idea of laser eye surgery. The thought of a laser going near my eye... I just can't do it. I'll stick to my contacts, even though they dry out my eyes so badly in the winter with the heating on. 

But, um... yeah. The issue is just that I can't focus. I'm so worried I'm messing up the numbers because I just can't keep my head straight, and that's what keeps me tossing and turning.

I just sort of sit on the couch when I get home.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired during the day?\n\nPatient: Tired? Oh, all the time. I mean, I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I just lie there in the dark, replaying all these client models in my head. Their previous advisor had them in these obscure mutual funds with exorbitant expense ratios. I mean, who puts a conservative growth client in a fund with a 1.5% fee? It's basically robbery. I spent three hours just reading the prospectus for one of them, and the fine print on the redemption fees was buried on page forty-two. Page forty-two! And the font was so small I actually had to go find my reading glasses, which I never usually wear. I bought them at a pharmacy in the airport in Atlanta two years ago when my flight got delayed because of a thunderstorm. The lightning was incredible, though, just lighting up the whole tarmac... \n\nBut anyway, I'll finally drift off and then I'm wide awake again at 3 or 4 in the morning. My stomach gets all tight, like a knot, and I just lie there until my alarm goes off. Um, no, not really. I mean, nothing like a car crash or a war or anything like that. Just the usual stress, I guess. Though, speaking of stress, the building management decided to test the fire alarms yesterday right in the middle of the afternoon. I had to close my blinds, but the blinds in my office are those cheap plastic vertical ones. Half the slats are missing the little clips at the top, so they just hang there crooked, and they don't even block the light properly. I put in a maintenance request for them six months ago. I don't know why anyone would want to use soap that burns... \n\nBut, um, no, no trauma. Just... just life being exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think is causing you to lie awake at night replaying work scenarios?\n\nPatient: Specific? I don't... Last week it was a tie with little rubber ducks all over it. And he pairs them with these short-sleeved button-down shirts that are always just a little too tight across the shoulders. Navy blue is authoritative but not screaming at you. I painted my dining room navy blue last spring. Well, technically it was called \"Midnight Sapphire,\" but it's navy. I bought the paint at this little independent hardware store near my house because I try to support local businesses, but the guy who mixed it didn't seal the lid properly. When I put it in the trunk of my car, it tipped over, and let me tell you, getting Midnight Sapphire out of beige carpet is impossible. I tried everything—club soda, vinegar, this industrial cleaner I ordered online that smelled like pine needles and ammonia. The smell was so strong I had to drive with the windows down for a week, and it was still April, so it was freezing. I ended up just buying a rubber cargo mat to cover the stain. It's black, which clashes with the beige, but what can you do? \n\nBut yeah, work is just... it's just heavy. All of it. I don't think one thing is worse than the rest.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about your job that you think is keeping you awake at night?\n\nPatient: Specific about the job? I... no, I don't think it's any one specific thing. It's just the whole environment, you know? It's like this low-level hum of anxiety that never stops. Actually, speaking of humming, the fluorescent light above my cubicle has been making this awful buzzing sound for three weeks. It's not loud, but it's just this constant *bzzzz* that burrows into your brain. I asked facilities to change the bulb, and they sent up this new kid. I think his name is Tyler. He couldn't have been older than twenty, and he was wearing these enormous headphones around his neck the whole time. Not even listening to them, just wearing them like a necklace. Who does that? And they were those bright green ones, like neon green. I don't understand this obsession with neon colors lately. I was at the grocery store yesterday—the one on 4th Street, not the big one out by the highway—and they had this entire display of neon-colored sports drinks. Blue raspberry, electric lemon... who wants to drink something that looks like antifreeze? I ended up just buying my usual sparkling water, but even that was a hassle because they rearranged the aisles. Why do they always rearrange the aisles? You finally learn where the oatmeal is, and suddenly it's in aisle seven next to the dog food. It makes no sense. I don't even have a dog. My sister has a dog, a golden retriever named Buster, and he sheds everywhere. She brought him over for Thanksgiving last year, and I was finding blonde hairs woven into my area rug until Easter... \n\nBut anyway, no. Nothing specific about the job. Just... everything. It just all stays with me when I close my eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself avoiding going into the office or certain work-related tasks because of feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Avoiding tasks? I... well, things are definitely slipping. My financial models, the slide decks for the presentations... I just can't seem to get them together anymore. I actually got a formal performance warning about it last week. HR handed it to me in this terrible, flimsy manila folder. I really don't understand why corporate offices still use manila folders. The color is just so depressing. It reminds me of the waiting room at my old dentist's office, Dr. Aris. He always had this faint smell of peppermint and latex, and he played smooth jazz in the background. Kenny G, mostly. I absolutely cannot stand the saxophone. It's just so... whiny. My nephew wanted to play the saxophone in middle school, but my sister made him take up the clarinet instead because it was cheaper to rent from the music shop. He quit after three weeks anyway. Kids just don't have any follow-through these days. He spends all his time on his phone now, watching other people play video games on the internet. I don't get that at all. Why watch someone else play a game when you could just play it yourself? \n\nBut... where was I? Right, the warning. Yeah, I'm just not keeping up. And it's not just the office. I used to have things that kept my life outside of work intact, you know? But I've completely stopped showing up for my volunteer teaching, and my Tuesday yoga class, and even my book club. I just don't go anymore. The book club host, Susan, always serves these terribly dry little cucumber sandwiches anyway, and she cuts the crusts off, which just seems incredibly wasteful... but yeah. I guess I am avoiding things. Everything, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep issues and stress at work are affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: My relationships? I... I don't really know. I guess I haven't seen anyone enough lately to even tell. I mean, my sister called me on Sunday, but I just let it go to voicemail. Her voicemails are always so long, anyway. She rambles on about her neighbor's landscaping or whatever new diet she's trying. The last one was the keto diet, I think? She was eating so much cheese, just endless blocks of cheddar. I don't know how anyone's digestion can handle that. I tried to make a cheese fondue once for a New Year's Eve party back in, oh, maybe 2015? I bought this beautiful cast-iron fondue pot, cherry red, very heavy. But I didn't get the heat right, and the cheese just separated into this oily, stringy mess. It was so embarrassing. Everyone pretended it was fine and just dipped their bread in the oil, but I knew. I ended up throwing the whole pot away because I couldn't get the burnt cheese off the bottom. I probably should have soaked it in baking soda, but I was just so frustrated. Baking soda is amazing, really. My grandmother used to keep an open box in the fridge to absorb odors, and I still do that. The yellow box, you know the one. \n\nBut... no, I haven't really noticed anything specific with my family or friends. I just don't have the energy to see them, so there's not much to affect, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning that you think might be causing you to wake up?\n\nPatient: Something specific before I wake up? I... no, not really. I mean, the reason I finally came in to see you is because I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I just lie awake for hours, replaying all my client models in my head. And then, without fail, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning, and my stomach is just tight, like a knot. It feels awful. It actually feels a lot like when I had food poisoning back in 2018. I had gone to this seafood place down by the harbor—the one with the blue awnings, not the red ones. I ordered the scallops, and I knew right away something was off because they smelled a little bit like ammonia, but I didn't want to make a fuss, so I ate them anyway. Big mistake. I spent the next two days curled up on my bathroom floor. The tile was so cold, but it actually felt kind of nice against my cheek. I have this really pretty seafoam green tile in my bathroom. I picked it out myself when I remodeled a few years ago. The contractor tried to talk me into white subway tile because it's \"classic,\" but I think white subway tile is just so boring. It looks like a subway station, obviously. Why would I want my bathroom to look like a public transit stop? The subway is always so dirty anyway, and it smells like ozone and old pretzels. \n\nBut... yeah, I just wake up with that tight stomach, and I can't go back to sleep. And then I have to drag myself to work feeling like I never actually rested at all. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think about right before you wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning?\n\nPatient: Something in particular I think about? I... I don't think so. I'm usually just asleep, and then I'm awake, and my stomach is doing that awful knot thing. It's not like I'm having a specific dream or anything. I don't really dream much anyway. Or maybe I just don't remember them. My friend Sarah used to keep a dream journal. She swore by it. She had this beautiful leather-bound notebook with handmade paper. You know the kind, with the little flower petals actually pressed into the pages? It looked so nice, but it was impossible to write on with a regular pen because the ink would bleed everywhere. She insisted on using a fountain pen, which just seemed incredibly messy to me. Fountain pens always leak in your bag. I ruined a perfectly good silk lining in a purse once because of a leaky pen. It was this gorgeous navy blue tote I got on sale at Nordstrom. I tried everything to get the stain out—hairspray, rubbing alcohol, even that expensive dry-cleaning fluid you can buy in the little bottles—but nothing worked. It just left this huge, dark blue smudge right at the bottom. I ended up having to throw the whole bag away, which was a shame because it had the perfect number of pockets. It's so hard to find a bag with good pockets these days. They either have none at all, and everything just falls into a black hole, or they have too many, and you can never remember where you put your keys. \n\nBut... no, I don't think there's any specific thought that wakes me up. It's just the waking up part that happens.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think is causing you the most anxiety or stress right before you go to bed?\n\nPatient: Right before bed? I... I'm not really sure. I don't think there's one specific thing. I usually just try to read a little bit to get my mind to quiet down, so I'm not really focusing on work or anything. I have this brass reading lamp clamped to my headboard. It's supposed to be one of those touch-activated ones, where you just tap the base and it changes brightness? But it's so finicky. Half the time I tap it and nothing happens, or I barely brush against it with my pillow and it turns up to the absolute brightest setting. It takes those weird little halogen bulbs, too, the ones you aren't supposed to touch with your bare hands because the oils from your skin make the glass shatter or burn out faster. I always have to use a tissue when I change them. I had to go to three different hardware stores just to find the right wattage last month. The one I finally found them at was this tiny mom-and-pop place downtown. It smelled exactly like my grandfather's garage—that very specific mix of sawdust, motor oil, and potting soil. He used to spend hours out there fixing up old radios. He had this massive wooden Zenith radio from the 1930s that he restored completely by hand. The dial lit up with this warm orange glow, and it took a full minute to warm up the tubes before you could hear anything but static. It was beautiful, really. I wonder whatever happened to that radio after he passed. My aunt probably sold it at an estate sale for practically nothing. She was always doing things like that, just getting rid of family history without asking anyone else if they wanted it. \n\nBut... anyway. No, I don't think there's a particular thought that stresses me out right before I go to sleep. I'm just so heavy and tired by that point, I just want the day to be over.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that happens during the day that you think might be contributing to your waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning?\n\nPatient: During the day? I... no, not that I've noticed. I mean, my days are all pretty much the exact same. I just sit at my desk looking at financial models and spreadsheets for eight or nine hours. It's just a blur of numbers, really. The only thing that really stands out is how freezing my office is. Building management refuses to adjust the thermostat on our floor, so I have to keep this huge, chunky wool cardigan draped over my chair. It's this really hideous mustard yellow color. I bought it at a thrift store up in Vermont when I went on a trip with my ex-husband, gosh, maybe ten years ago now. We went up there to look at the fall foliage, which was supposed to be relaxing, but we spent the entire weekend arguing because he absolutely refused to use the GPS on his phone. He bought this massive, fold-out paper map at a gas station and insisted on navigating with that because he said it was more authentic. We ended up getting completely lost on this awful dirt logging road for three hours. The rental car got completely coated in mud. It was a silver sedan, and by the time we got back to the highway, it was entirely brown. I remember the guy at the rental return desk gave us the dirtiest look. I always try to return rental cars clean, you know? It just seems polite. I usually even take out the trash from the little side pockets in the doors. People always leave gum wrappers and old receipts in there. \n\nBut... yeah, sorry. No, there's nothing specific during the day that I think causes the waking up. It's just the same dull routine, and then I still end up awake in the middle of the night regardless.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think about right before you fall asleep that might be related to your waking up at 3 or 4 in the morning?\n\nPatient: Right before I fall asleep? Um, no. I don't really think about much of anything. Like I said, I'm usually just reading until my eyes get too heavy to keep open. I've been trying to get through this biography of Marie Antoinette for, honestly, probably six months now. It's so dense. The author spends like three whole chapters just describing the architecture of Versailles and the specific types of silk they imported for the curtains. Which is interesting, I guess, but it makes me so sleepy. I actually went to Versailles once, right after college. I went backpacking through Europe with my roommate, Claire. We took the train out from Paris, and it was pouring rain the entire day. We didn't even get to see the gardens properly because the mud was so thick it was ruining our shoes. I was wearing these cheap canvas sneakers I bought at a market in London, and the water just soaked right through. My feet were freezing. We ended up spending way too much money on hot chocolate in the cafe just to warm up. They served it in these tiny, delicate porcelain cups with little gold rims. I was terrified I was going to drop it and break it, because my hands were shaking so much from the cold. The hot chocolate was so rich, though, almost like drinking melted candy bars. I haven't had hot chocolate like that since. The powdered stuff you buy at the grocery store here just tastes like dusty sugar water in comparison. You have to use actual milk, not just hot water, but even then, it's never the same. \n\nBut, um... no. To answer your question, there's no specific thought right before I fall asleep. I just kind of drift off while I'm reading, and then a few hours later, my eyes are just wide open again.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think about right before you fall asleep that might be related to your work?\n\nPatient: Right before I fall asleep? Well, I guess... yeah, actually. I came in because I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I'll lie there, and instead of reading, I just start replaying all the client models from the day in my head. Just staring at the ceiling, thinking about the spreadsheets. And then I finally drift off, but I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach completely tight. It's awful. I end up going to work feeling like I never actually rested at all. I have to take the subway to work, and it's always so crowded in the mornings. You're just packed in there like sardines. The other day, this man next to me was holding a cup of coffee and the train jerked, and he spilled it all over the floor. It was one of those iced coffees with the plastic cups, and the lid just popped right off. The ice cubes were sliding all over the floor every time the train stopped or started. Nobody said anything, we all just kind of stared at the ice cubes sliding back and forth. It reminded me of playing air hockey when I was a kid. My brother and I used to go to this arcade down the street from our house on Saturday afternoons. He was always so much better at it than me, he'd slam the puck so hard it would fly off the table and hit someone. He actually chipped a tooth once because he leaned too far over the table and the puck bounced up and hit him right in the mouth. My mother was furious. She made him pay for the dental bill out of his allowance, which took him almost a year to pay off. He was so mad about it, he refused to speak to her for a week. \n\nBut, um, yes. I do think about work right before I fall asleep. I just replay those client models over and over, and then I'm up at 3 AM with my stomach in knots.\n\nDoctor: Is there a particular work-related issue that you think is most responsible for keeping you awake at night?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's just that I can't seem to hold onto my thoughts anymore. Like, spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me now take me hours. I just completely lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll sit there and reread the same financial assumptions over and over again, and I still worry I've missed something obvious before I have to do a presentation. It's exhausting. I used to be so good at presentations. I remember this one time, maybe five years ago, I had to present to the board of this huge tech startup. We were in this massive glass conference room on the 40th floor, looking out over the whole city. They had catered lunch, and it wasn't just regular sandwiches, it was this incredible sushi spread. Like, really high-end stuff. I ate so much spicy tuna I thought I was going to be sick before I even stood up to speak. I actually dropped a piece of ginger on my blouse right before we started. It was a white silk blouse, too, the kind you have to dry clean. I tried to dab it with a napkin, but it just left this faint pink smudge right near the collar. I spent the whole presentation trying to angle my body so nobody would see it. The CEO was this guy who wore those expensive rimless glasses, and he kept squinting at me. I thought he was looking at the stain, but it turned out he just needed a new prescription. He told me afterward he was getting laser eye surgery the next week. I've always been terrified of the idea of laser eye surgery. The thought of a laser going near my eye... I just can't do it. I'll stick to my contacts, even though they dry out my eyes so badly in the winter with the heating on. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The issue is just that I can't focus. I'm so worried I'm messing up the numbers because I just can't keep my head straight, and that's what keeps me tossing and turning.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it's... it's really affecting everything now. I mean, at work, it's gotten so bad that I actually just got a formal performance warning. My boss pulled me in because my financial models and my presentations just keep slipping, and I'm making too many mistakes. But it's definitely not just the office. My next-door neighbor tried to grow a vegetable garden last summer, mostly heirloom tomatoes, and the squirrels just absolutely decimated it. They would take exactly one bite out of a perfectly ripe, red tomato and then just leave it sitting on the sidewalk. It drove him crazy. He ended up buying this giant plastic owl to put on his fence to scare them away. But the squirrels figured it out in about two days, and they just started sitting right on the owl's head to eat the tomatoes. He was so mad he tore the whole garden down and just put down gravel. 

But, um... anyway, yes. It's generalized, I guess you'd say. Between the formal warning at work and completely dropping out of the teaching and the yoga and the book club... everything is just falling apart.

The author spends like three whole chapters just describing the architecture of Versailles and the specific types of silk they imported for the curtains. Which is interesting, I guess, but it makes me so sleepy. I actually went to Versailles once, right after college. I went backpacking through Europe with my roommate, Claire. We took the train out from Paris, and it was pouring rain the entire day. We didn't even get to see the gardens properly because the mud was so thick it was ruining our shoes. I was wearing these cheap canvas sneakers I bought at a market in London, and the water just soaked right through. My feet were freezing. We ended up spending way too much money on hot chocolate in the cafe just to warm up. They served it in these tiny, delicate porcelain cups with little gold rims. I was terrified I was going to drop it and break it, because my hands were shaking so much from the cold. The hot chocolate was so rich, though, almost like drinking melted candy bars. I haven't had hot chocolate like that since. The powdered stuff you buy at the grocery store here just tastes like dusty sugar water in comparison. You have to use actual milk, not just hot water, but even then, it's never the same. \n\nBut, um... no. To answer your question, there's no specific thought right before I fall asleep. I just kind of drift off while I'm reading, and then a few hours later, my eyes are just wide open again.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think about right before you fall asleep that might be related to your work?\n\nPatient: Right before I fall asleep? Well, I guess... yeah, actually. I came in because I barely sleep in any useful way anymore. I'll lie there, and instead of reading, I just start replaying all the client models from the day in my head. Just staring at the ceiling, thinking about the spreadsheets. And then I finally drift off, but I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach completely tight. It's awful. I end up going to work feeling like I never actually rested at all. I have to take the subway to work, and it's always so crowded in the mornings. You're just packed in there like sardines. The other day, this man next to me was holding a cup of coffee and the train jerked, and he spilled it all over the floor. It was one of those iced coffees with the plastic cups, and the lid just popped right off. The ice cubes were sliding all over the floor every time the train stopped or started. Nobody said anything, we all just kind of stared at the ice cubes sliding back and forth. It reminded me of playing air hockey when I was a kid. My brother and I used to go to this arcade down the street from our house on Saturday afternoons. He was always so much better at it than me, he'd slam the puck so hard it would fly off the table and hit someone. He actually chipped a tooth once because he leaned too far over the table and the puck bounced up and hit him right in the mouth. My mother was furious. She made him pay for the dental bill out of his allowance, which took him almost a year to pay off. He was so mad about it, he refused to speak to her for a week. \n\nBut, um, yes. I do think about work right before I fall asleep. I just replay those client models over and over, and then I'm up at 3 AM with my stomach in knots.\n\nDoctor: Is there a particular work-related issue that you think is most responsible for keeping you awake at night?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's just that I can't seem to hold onto my thoughts anymore. Like, spreadsheets that used to be second nature to me now take me hours. I just completely lose the thread of what I'm doing. I'll sit there and reread the same financial assumptions over and over again, and I still worry I've missed something obvious before I have to do a presentation. It's exhausting. I used to be so good at presentations. I remember this one time, maybe five years ago, I had to present to the board of this huge tech startup. We were in this massive glass conference room on the 40th floor, looking out over the whole city. They had catered lunch, and it wasn't just regular sandwiches, it was this incredible sushi spread. Like, really high-end stuff. I ate so much spicy tuna I thought I was going to be sick before I even stood up to speak. I actually dropped a piece of ginger on my blouse right before we started. It was a white silk blouse, too, the kind you have to dry clean. I tried to dab it with a napkin, but it just left this faint pink smudge right near the collar. I spent the whole presentation trying to angle my body so nobody would see it. The CEO was this guy who wore those expensive rimless glasses, and he kept squinting at me. I thought he was looking at the stain, but it turned out he just needed a new prescription. He told me afterward he was getting laser eye surgery the next week. I've always been terrified of the idea of laser eye surgery. The thought of a laser going near my eye... I just can't do it. I'll stick to my contacts, even though they dry out my eyes so badly in the winter with the heating on. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The issue is just that I can't focus. I'm so worried I'm messing up the numbers because I just can't keep my head straight, and that's what keeps me tossing and turning.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep issues and anxiety are directly related to your work stress or are they more generalized and affecting other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's really affecting everything now. I mean, at work, it's gotten so bad that I actually just got a formal performance warning. My boss pulled me in because my financial models and my presentations just keep slipping, and I'm making too many mistakes. But it's definitely not just the office. I’ve completely stopped showing up for the things that used to keep my life outside of work intact. Like, I used to do this volunteer teaching on the weekends, and I went to yoga, and I had my monthly book club. I haven't been to book club in months. \n\nThe last time I went, we met at my friend Susan's house, and she made these incredible baked brie bites with homemade fig jam. She actually grows her own figs in her backyard, which I didn't even know you could do in this climate. Apparently, you have to wrap the whole tree in burlap during the winter so it doesn't freeze. My next-door neighbor tried to grow a vegetable garden last summer, mostly heirloom tomatoes, and the squirrels just absolutely decimated it. They would take exactly one bite out of a perfectly ripe, red tomato and then just leave it sitting on the sidewalk. It drove him crazy. He ended up buying this giant plastic owl to put on his fence to scare them away. But the squirrels figured it out in about two days, and they just started sitting right on the owl's head to eat the tomatoes. He was so mad he tore the whole garden down and just put down gravel. \n\nBut, um... anyway, yes. It's generalized, I guess you'd say. Between the formal warning at work and completely dropping out of the teaching and the yoga and the book club... everything is just falling apart."
